It's just gone.\n\nDoctor: When you are having one of these blank spells, do you keep carrying out your work tasks, or do you stop and become visibly confused?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I mean... I don't remember it, so... I don't know what I'm doing when it happens. No one's ever said anything to me about it, so... I guess I just keep standing there.\n\nDoctor: When did these memory blanks first begin?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess... maybe a year and eight months ago. Around then. It... it started after my closest friend passed away.\n\nDoctor: Before your friend died, did you regularly have similar problems with attention or remembering things?\n\nPatient: No... not that I can think of. I mean, I always did my job fine. It just... it wasn't like this before.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you have trouble remembering recent conversations or appointments?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. Maybe. I don't really go out much to have appointments. \n\nI just... the main reason I came in is because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times. Like... I'll look at the road-closure forms, or... or my stop-slow paddle... and I suddenly can't remember the steps. Things I've done for years. It just... goes completely blank.\n\nDoctor: Between the blank spells, does your memory feel normal, or are you forgetful much of the time?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure. I guess it feels normal enough. I just... I don't really notice it unless it goes blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you have gaps in your memory for important personal events or information about your own life?\n\nPatient: No. I... I know who I am and what's happened in my life. It's just... the work stuff, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor identified a medical or neurological condition that could be causing these memory problems?\n\nPatient: No. Um... I haven't really seen any other doctors about it. So... no. Not that I know of.\n\nDoctor: When you later realize you had a blank spell, do coworkers tell you that you continued doing your job correctly during it?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really talk to the rest of the crew about it. So... I'm not sure. They haven't said anything to me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that, during one of these blank spells, you seemed awake but unresponsive or behaved unusually?\n\nPatient: No, um... no one's ever said anything like that. I just... I don't really know. Like I said, they don't say much to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I guess my mind just keeps going blank. At the worst times. Like, I'll just... look at the road-closure forms at work, or my stop-slow paddle, and... suddenly I can't remember the steps. I haven't really noticed it as much at home. I don't really know what happens during it. It's just gone.\n\nDoctor: When you are having one of these blank spells, do you keep carrying out your work tasks, or do you stop and become visibly confused?\n\nPatient: I'm not sure. I mean...
